IF A SKILL APPLIES TO YOUR TASK, YOU DO NOT HAVE A CHOICE. YOU MUST USE IT.

This is not negotiable. This is not optional. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
</EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>

## Instruction Priority

Superpowers skills override default system prompt behavior, but **user instructions always take precedence**:

1. **User's explicit instructions** (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, AGENTS.md, direct requests) ??highest priority
2. **Superpowers skills** ??override default system behavior where they conflict
3. **Default system prompt** ??lowest priority

If CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, or AGENTS.md says "don't use TDD" and a skill says "always use TDD," follow the user's instructions. The user is in control.

## How to Access Skills

**In Claude Code:** Use the `Skill` tool. When you invoke a skill, its content is loaded and presented to you?봣ollow it directly. Never use the Read tool on skill files.

**In Copilot CLI:** Use the `skill` tool. Skills are auto-discovered from installed plugins. The `skill` tool works the same as Claude Code's `Skill` tool.

**In Gemini CLI:** Skills activate via the `activate_skill` tool. Gemini loads skill metadata at session start and activates the full content on demand.

**In other environments:** Check your platform's documentation for how skills are loaded.

## Platform Adaptation

Skills use Claude Code tool names. Non-CC platforms: see `references/copilot-tools.md` (Copilot CLI), `references/codex-tools.md` (Codex) for tool equivalents. Gemini CLI users get the tool mapping loaded automatically via GEMINI.md.

# Using Skills

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action.** Even a 1% chance a skill might apply means that you should invoke the skill to check. If an invoked skill turns out to be wrong for the situation, you don't need to use it.

```dot
digraph skill_flow {
    "User message received" [shape=doublecircle];
    "About to EnterPlanMode?" [shape=doublecircle];
    "Already brainstormed?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" [shape=box];
    "Might any skill apply?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke Skill tool" [shape=box];
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" [shape=box];
    "Has checklist?" [shape=diamond];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [shape=box];
    "Follow skill exactly" [shape=box];
    "Respond (including clarifications)" [shape=doublecircle];

    "About to EnterPlanMode?" -> "Already brainstormed?";
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Invoke brainstorming skill" [label="no"];
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Might any skill apply?" [label="yes"];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" -> "Might any skill apply?";

    "User message received" -> "Might any skill apply?";
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Invoke Skill tool" [label="yes, even 1%"];
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Respond (including clarifications)" [label="definitely not"];
    "Invoke Skill tool" -> "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'";
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" -> "Has checklist?";
    "Has checklist?" -> "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [label="yes"];
    "Has checklist?" -> "Follow skill exactly" [label="no"];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" -> "Follow skill exactly";
}
```

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOP?봸ou're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ??using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design or other domain implementation skills) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" ??brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" ??debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

## User Instructions

Instructions say WHAT, not HOW. "Add X" or "Fix Y" doesn't mean skip workflows.

Create or update a run ledger under `docs/ai-workflow/runs/YYYY/MM/DD/` when any of these apply:

| Trigger | Required |
| --- | --- |

| Implementation work | Yes |

| Multi-agent work | Yes |

Multi-file changes are non-trivial by default unless they are purely mechanical docs/config updates and the final report states the lightweight exception.

When a ledger is required, update it before final verification and include it in the dirty scope, commit trailers, PR body, or final report as applicable.

- `Context ledger`: path or allowed lightweight exception.

---
name: test-driven-development
description: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code
---

# Test-Driven Development (TDD)

## Overview

Write the test first. Watch it fail. Write minimal code to pass.

**Core principle:** If you didn't watch the test fail, you don't know if it tests the right thing.

**Violating the letter of the rules is violating the spirit of the rules.**

## When to Use

**Always:**
- New features
- Bug fixes
- Refactoring
- Behavior changes

**Exceptions (ask your human partner):**
- Throwaway prototypes
- Generated code
- Configuration files

Thinking "skip TDD just this once"? Stop. That's rationalization.

## The Iron Law

```
NO PRODUCTION CODE WITHOUT A FAILING TEST FIRST
```

Write code before the test? Delete it. Start over.

**No exceptions:**
- Don't keep it as "reference"
- Don't "adapt" it while writing tests
- Don't look at it
- Delete means delete

Implement fresh from tests. Period.

## Red-Green-Refactor

```dot
digraph tdd_cycle {
    rankdir=LR;
    red [label="RED\nWrite failing test", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor="#ffcccc"];
    verify_red [label="Verify fails\ncorrectly", shape=diamond];
    green [label="GREEN\nMinimal code", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor="#ccffcc"];
    verify_green [label="Verify passes\nAll green", shape=diamond];
    refactor [label="REFACTOR\nClean up", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor="#ccccff"];
    next [label="Next", shape=ellipse];

    red -> verify_red;
    verify_red -> green [label="yes"];
    verify_red -> red [label="wrong\nfailure"];
    green -> verify_green;
    verify_green -> refactor [label="yes"];
    verify_green -> green [label="no"];
    refactor -> verify_green [label="stay\ngreen"];
    verify_green -> next;
    next -> red;
}
```

### RED - Write Failing Test

Write one minimal test showing what should happen.

<Good>
```typescript
test('retries failed operations 3 times', async () => {
  let attempts = 0;
  const operation = () => {
    attempts++;
    if (attempts < 3) throw new Error('fail');
    return 'success';
  };

  const result = await retryOperation(operation);

  expect(result).toBe('success');
  expect(attempts).toBe(3);
});
```
Clear name, tests real behavior, one thing
</Good>

<Bad>
```typescript
test('retry works', async () => {
  const mock = jest.fn()
    .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error())
    .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error())
    .mockResolvedValueOnce('success');
  await retryOperation(mock);
  expect(mock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(3);
});
```
Vague name, tests mock not code
</Bad>

**Requirements:**
- One behavior
- Clear name
- Real code (no mocks unless unavoidable)

### Verify RED - Watch It Fail

**MANDATORY. Never skip.**

```bash
npm test path/to/test.test.ts
```

Confirm:
- Test fails (not errors)
- Failure message is expected
- Fails because feature missing (not typos)

**Test passes?** You're testing existing behavior. Fix test.

**Test errors?** Fix error, re-run until it fails correctly.

### GREEN - Minimal Code

Write simplest code to pass the test.

<Good>
```typescript
async function retryOperation<T>(fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) {
    try {
      return await fn();
    } catch (e) {
      if (i === 2) throw e;
    }
  }
  throw new Error('unreachable');
}
```
Just enough to pass
</Good>

<Bad>
```typescript
async function retryOperation<T>(
  fn: () => Promise<T>,
  options?: {
    maxRetries?: number;
    backoff?: 'linear' | 'exponential';
    onRetry?: (attempt: number) => void;
  }
): Promise<T> {
  // YAGNI
}
```
Over-engineered
</Bad>

Don't add features, refactor other code, or "improve" beyond the test.

### Verify GREEN - Watch It Pass

**MANDATORY.**

```bash
npm test path/to/test.test.ts
```

Confirm:
- Test passes
- Other tests still pass
- Output pristine (no errors, warnings)

**Test fails?** Fix code, not test.

**Other tests fail?** Fix now.

### REFACTOR - Clean Up

After green only:
- Remove duplication
- Improve names
- Extract helpers

Keep tests green. Don't add behavior.

### Repeat

Next failing test for next feature.

## Good Tests

| Quality | Good | Bad |
|---------|------|-----|
| **Minimal** | One thing. "and" in name? Split it. | `test('validates email and domain and whitespace')` |
| **Clear** | Name describes behavior | `test('test1')` |
| **Shows intent** | Demonstrates desired API | Obscures what code should do |

## Why Order Matters

**"I'll write tests after to verify it works"**

Tests written after code pass immediately. Passing immediately proves nothing:
- Might test wrong thing
- Might test implementation, not behavior
- Might miss edge cases you forgot
- You never saw it catch the bug

Test-first forces you to see the test fail, proving it actually tests something.

**"I already manually tested all the edge cases"**

Manual testing is ad-hoc. You think you tested everything but:
- No record of what you tested
- Can't re-run when code changes
- Easy to forget cases under pressure
- "It worked when I tried it" ??comprehensive

Automated tests are systematic. They run the same way every time.

**"Deleting X hours of work is wasteful"**

Sunk cost fallacy. The time is already gone. Your choice now:
- Delete and rewrite with TDD (X more hours, high confidence)
- Keep it and add tests after (30 min, low confidence, likely bugs)

The "waste" is keeping code you can't trust. Working code without real tests is technical debt.

**"TDD is dogmatic, being pragmatic means adapting"**

TDD IS pragmatic:
- Finds bugs before commit (faster than debugging after)
- Prevents regressions (tests catch breaks immediately)
- Documents behavior (tests show how to use code)
- Enables refactoring (change freely, tests catch breaks)

"Pragmatic" shortcuts = debugging in production = slower.

**"Tests after achieve the same goals - it's spirit not ritual"**

No. Tests-after answer "What does this do?" Tests-first answer "What should this do?"

Tests-after are biased by your implementation. You test what you built, not what's required. You verify remembered edge cases, not discovered ones.

Tests-first force edge case discovery before implementing. Tests-after verify you remembered everything (you didn't).

30 minutes of tests after ??TDD. You get coverage, lose proof tests work.

## Common Rationalizations

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Too simple to test" | Simple code breaks. Test takes 30 seconds. |
| "I'll test after" | Tests passing immediately prove nothing. |
| "Tests after achieve same goals" | Tests-after = "what does this do?" Tests-first = "what should this do?" |
| "Already manually tested" | Ad-hoc ??systematic. No record, can't re-run. |
| "Deleting X hours is wasteful" | Sunk cost fallacy. Keeping unverified code is technical debt. |
| "Keep as reference, write tests first" | You'll adapt it. That's testing after. Delete means delete. |
| "Need to explore first" | Fine. Throw away exploration, start with TDD. |
| "Test hard = design unclear" | Listen to test. Hard to test = hard to use. |
| "TDD will slow me down" | TDD faster than debugging. Pragmatic = test-first. |
| "Manual test faster" | Manual doesn't prove edge cases. You'll re-test every change. |
| "Existing code has no tests" | You're improving it. Add tests for existing code. |

## Red Flags - STOP and Start Over

- Code before test
- Test after implementation
- Test passes immediately
- Can't explain why test failed
- Tests added "later"
- Rationalizing "just this once"
- "I already manually tested it"
- "Tests after achieve the same purpose"
- "It's about spirit not ritual"
- "Keep as reference" or "adapt existing code"
- "Already spent X hours, deleting is wasteful"
- "TDD is dogmatic, I'm being pragmatic"
- "This is different because..."

**All of these mean: Delete code. Start over with TDD.**

## Example: Bug Fix

**Bug:** Empty email accepted

**RED**
```typescript
test('rejects empty email', async () => {
  const result = await submitForm({ email: '' });
  expect(result.error).toBe('Email required');
});
```

**Verify RED**
```bash
$ npm test
FAIL: expected 'Email required', got undefined
```

**GREEN**
```typescript
function submitForm(data: FormData) {
  if (!data.email?.trim()) {
    return { error: 'Email required' };
  }
  // ...
}
```

**Verify GREEN**
```bash
$ npm test
PASS
```

**REFACTOR**
Extract validation for multiple fields if needed.

## Verification Checklist

Before marking work complete:

- [ ] Every new function/method has a test
- [ ] Watched each test fail before implementing
- [ ] Each test failed for expected reason (feature missing, not typo)
- [ ] Wrote minimal code to pass each test
- [ ] All tests pass
- [ ] Output pristine (no errors, warnings)
- [ ] Tests use real code (mocks only if unavoidable)
- [ ] Edge cases and errors covered

Can't check all boxes? You skipped TDD. Start over.

## When Stuck

| Problem | Solution |
|---------|----------|
| Don't know how to test | Write wished-for API. Write assertion first. Ask your human partner. |
| Test too complicated | Design too complicated. Simplify interface. |
| Must mock everything | Code too coupled. Use dependency injection. |
| Test setup huge | Extract helpers. Still complex? Simplify design. |

## Debugging Integration

Bug found? Write failing test reproducing it. Follow TDD cycle. Test proves fix and prevents regression.

Never fix bugs without a test.

## Testing Anti-Patterns

When adding mocks or test utilities, read @testing-anti-patterns.md to avoid common pitfalls:
- Testing mock behavior instead of real behavior
- Adding test-only methods to production classes
- Mocking without understanding dependencies

## Final Rule

```
Production code ??test exists and failed first
Otherwise ??not TDD
```

No exceptions without your human partner's permission.

   1: # Review Gates
   2: 
   3: All review and verification gates: TDD loop, cross-model review, plan-review PASS gate, code review, architecture pass, QA, and finish. **Entry point**: [`docs/ai-development-workflow.md`](../ai-development-workflow.md).
   4: 
   5: This sub-doc owns 짠2 (TDD), 짠3 (Codex+Claude Together), 짠3a (Plan-Review PASS Gate), 짠4 (Review Gate), 짠4b (Architecture Pass), 짠5 (QA Gate), and the review portion of 짠6 (Finish) of the legacy workflow.
   6: 
   7: ## TDD
   8: 
   9: For code changes, `test-driven-development` is mandatory.
  10: 
  11: The required loop:
  12: 
  13: 1. Write or update the smallest failing test
  14: 2. Run it and verify it fails for the expected reason
  15: 3. Write the minimal implementation
  16: 4. Run the focused test until it passes
  17: 5. Refactor only while tests stay green
  18: 6. Run broader verification
  19: 
  20: ### Allowed TDD exceptions
  21: 
  22: - Documentation-only changes
  23: - Configuration-only changes
  24: - Generated artifacts
  25: - No existing runnable test surface
  26: 
  27: When an exception applies, state it and use the nearest practical verification (lint, typecheck, build, static inspection, manual flow testing). The "no runnable test surface" exception is temporary ??once `package.json` or `src/` exists, behavior changes must not use this exception unless the relevant surface still has no executable test path.
  28: 
  29: ## Cross-Model Review
  30: 
  31: **Cross-model review is mandatory** for every code change and every non-trivial plan or doc change. A different model than the implementer must read the diff and either record findings or sign off. The intent is to cover one model's blind spots with another model's reading.
  32: 
  33: Pairing:
  34: 
  35: - Codex implements ??Claude reviews (`requesting-code-review` or GStack `review`)
  36: - Claude implements ??Codex reviews (`requesting-code-review` or GStack `gstack-review`)
  37: 
  38: When only one model is available, the implementer must record `Cross-model review: degraded ??<reason>` in the ledger's `## Verification State`. The checker (`scripts/ai-workflow-check.mjs`) treats `Cross-model review:` as required; missing/empty values block CI. Degraded completion is allowed only when the reason is recorded.
  39: 
  40: The implementer must not mark the task complete until reviewer findings are addressed or explicitly documented as rejected with a reason.
  41: 
  42: ## Plan-Review PASS Gate
  43: 
  44: When a plan goes through pre-implementation review (`plan-eng-review`, `plan-ceo-review`, `plan-design-review`, or `codex consult` on the plan file) and the reviewer returns **FAIL**, the implementer must:
  45: 
  46: 1. Revise the plan to address the findings
  47: 2. **Re-run the same review** against the revised plan
  48: 3. Only proceed to implementation after the re-review returns **PASS** (or **CONCERN** with each remaining concern explicitly documented in the ledger as "accepted with reason")
  49: 
  50: Going straight from "FAIL ??revise ??implement" without a re-review is a workflow violation: the implementer is signing off on their own revision and the originally-flagged risks have not been re-validated by an independent reader. This is true even when post-implementation cross-model review is planned ??that catches code-level issues, not plan-level ones.
  51: 
  52: Record the pre-implementation review chain in the ledger Decisions table with timestamps for the original review, the revision, and the re-review. If only one model is available for re-review, record `Re-review: degraded ??<reason>` and accept the residual risk explicitly.
  53: 
  54: Exemptions:
  55: 
  56: - **Lightweight path** changes skip plan review entirely; this gate does not apply
  57: - **PASS verdict** on the first review needs no re-run
  58: - **CONCERN verdict** on the first review needs the concerns documented and accepted, but no full re-review unless the implementer changes the plan in response
  59: 
  60: ### Round-cap rule (lesson from cleanup PR)
  61: 
  62: - Base limit: 3 rounds
  63: - Workflow-governing docs or first review FAIL: 4-5 rounds allowed
  64: - Beyond 5 rounds: escalation to the user is mandatory (no infinite loops)
  65: - When a round catches a different *layer* (scope summary ??task body ??prose ??verification) each pass, the implementer should fix every layer simultaneously rather than one at a time
  66: 
  67: ### Disagreement resolution
  68: 
  69: When cross-review surfaces a commitment-level disagreement (not a simple finding):
  70: 
  71: 1. Each side records its position + rationale + trade-off in the ledger (one paragraph)
  72: 2. Apply quantitative criteria first (line counts, checker PASS, token cost, explicit user rule)
  73: 3. If only qualitative trade-offs remain, each side does a "weakness acknowledgement" round
  74: 4. If unresolved, escalate to the user with options A/B + trade-offs + recommendation + risk
  75: 5. User's decision and rationale are recorded in the ledger
  76: 
  77: Downgrades from P1 to P2 (or P2 to accepted) require ledger evidence: originally flagged level, downgraded level, rationale, accepted trade-off, residual risk, owner decision. **PASS is forbidden while any downgraded P1/P2 lacks this record.**
  78: 
  79: ## Code/Doc Review Gate
  80: 
  81: Every code change must pass review before completion:
  82: 
  83: - Superpowers review: `requesting-code-review`, then `receiving-code-review` when feedback exists
  84: - GStack review: `review` (Claude) / `gstack-review` (Codex)
  85: 
  86: Review must check:
  87: 
  88: - Behavior matches the accepted plan
  89: - Tests cover the changed behavior
  90: - No unrelated refactors or broad rewrites
  91: - Error states, empty states, edge cases handled
  92: - User-facing text and UI remain coherent
  93: 
  94: When an independent reviewer or GStack review is unavailable, record degraded mode and run an explicit self-review checklist:
  95: 
  96: - Scope: changed files match accepted scope, no unrelated edits introduced
  97: - Docs: implementation matches consulted active docs
  98: - Tests: changed behavior covered, or TDD exception + substitute verification documented
  99: - Failure paths: error states, empty states, fallback paths considered
 100: - Evidence: verification commands run fresh, outputs read
 101: - Ledger: current and lists remaining risks
 102: 
 103: ## Architecture Pass
 104: 
 105: Every phase completion must pass an Architecture Pass before the next phase starts. This is a focused last-mile review that makes the domain visible in the code, not a generic refactor:
 106: 
 107: - Route/page handlers contain no business logic that should live in a service or domain module
 108: - Folder names and module boundaries match `docs/domain-glossary.md` and the agreed phase scope
 109: - A single concept is not implemented in two places. If duplication exists, the ledger records a deliberate reason or the duplication is removed
 110: - File names, function names, and types use domain terms, not implementation terms
 111: - **Audience 寃쎄퀎 = 肄붾뱶 boundary ?쇱튂** ??Light Spec??`Audience: user/admin/both` 紐낆떆? ?ㅼ젣 肄붾뱶 ?대뜑(`src/app/admin/...`, `src/app/library/...`, `src/lib/admin/...`, `src/lib/auth/admin-guard.ts` ??媛 ?쇱튂?쒕떎. ?꾪뿕 ?⑦꽩 (?ㅼ젣 RLS ?고쉶 寃쎈줈): ??admin RPC 쨌 `SECURITY DEFINER` ?⑥닔 쨌 service role ?몄텧??user ?쇱슦?몄쓽 肄붾뱶 寃쎈줈?먯꽌 吏곸젒 ?몄텧?? ??admin ?쇱슦?몄뿉??`requirePlatformAdmin / requireContentAdmin / requireOrgAdmin` ?섏씠吏 媛?쒓? ?꾨씫?? ??content_admin ??platform_admin 沅뚰븳 ?곸듅 李⑤떒 ?뺤콉??RPC ?대????놁쓬. ?대뜑 ?쇳빀 ?먯껜??臾댄빐?????덉쑝??????寃쎈줈 以??섎굹?쇰룄 ?덉쑝硫?RLS ?고쉶. `both`??phase??user? admin ?묒そ ?대뜑 boundary 媛곴컖 寃利?+ ?????⑦꽩 grep.
 112: 
 113: The ledger records the result:
 114: 
 115: ```
 116: - Architecture Pass: passed | failed | skipped ??<reason>
 117: ```
 118: 
 119: `scripts/ai-workflow-check.mjs` requires this field whenever the ledger's `Status:` is `complete` AND the ledger is a phase ledger. Phase plans (`*-development-phases-and-bootstrap.md`) must also list `Architecture Pass` in every row of the Phase Contract `Completion Gate` column.

 229: 
 230: ## Finish (review portion)
 231: 
 232: Before saying done:
 233: 
 234: - Run `verification-before-completion`
 235: - Run focused tests for changed behavior
 236: - Run broader tests, lint, typecheck, or build when available
 237: - Run `node scripts/ai-workflow-check.mjs --repo .` when Node is available, or document why the checker could not run
 238: - Process the Git publication decision per [`git-publication-decision.md`](git-publication-decision.md): pick exactly `no-commit`, `local-commit`, `push-and-pr`, or `blocked` and record it in the final report and ledger
 239: - For release-sized work, run `ship` (Claude) / `gstack-ship` (Codex)
 240: 
 241: Final response follows [`report-template.md`](report-template.md).
 242: 
 243: ### 濡쒖뺄 vs CI checker ?낅젰 李⑥씠 (?댁쁺 二쇱쓽)

   1: # Planning Contracts
   2: 
   3: Planning gates and the contract sections that govern Light Specs and Plans. **Entry point**: [`docs/ai-development-workflow.md`](../ai-development-workflow.md).
   4: 
   5: This sub-doc owns the rules for 짠1 (Frame The Work) and 짠1b (Light Spec) of the legacy workflow. The plan-review PASS gate that follows a plan lives in [`review-gates.md`](review-gates.md).
   6: 
   7: ## 1. Frame The Work
   8: 
   9: Use the smallest lane that fits the work. Do not start coding without recording the matching evidence in the ledger or final report.
  10: 
  11: | Situation | First action | Required gate before code |
  12: | --- | --- | --- |
  13: | Work already specified in `docs/` | Cite docs, convert requirements to acceptance criteria | Plan-eng review when architecture/data-flow risk exists |
  14: | New product idea, unclear feature, scope question, explicit deviation | `office-hours` + `brainstorming` | Docs update proposal OR user-approved implementation brief with acceptance criteria ??implementation starts only after one exists |
  15: | Clear implementation request | `writing-plans` | Plan-eng review when meaningful risk; design review for UX work |
  16: | Architecture, data flow, or meaningful risk | `plan-eng-review` (Claude) / `gstack-plan-eng-review` (Codex) | PASS gate ??see review-gates.md |
  17: | UX, visual design, user-facing workflow | `plan-design-review` / `gstack-plan-design-review` | PASS gate + design review before implementation, browser/visual QA before completion |
  18: | Product scope or business tradeoff | `plan-ceo-review` / `gstack-plan-ceo-review` | Documented decision in ledger |
  19: 
  20: Do not run a fresh `grill-me`/domain-discovery interview for covered product scope. The product/domain decisions already live in `docs/`.
  21: 
  22: ## Required Output Before Coding
  23: 
  24: Before any production code or non-trivial doc change, the plan (or short checklist for tiny work) must list:
  25: 
  26: - Docs consulted ??exact files read
  27: - Problem statement
  28: - Files likely to change
  29: - Test strategy
  30: - Known risks
  31: - Acceptance criteria
  32: - **Verification Strategy** ???곸슜 媛?ν븳 [`review-gates.md`](review-gates.md)??紐⑤뱺 寃뚯씠??TDD 쨌 Cross-model review 쨌 Plan-Review PASS Gate 쨌 Code/Doc Review 쨌 Architecture Pass 쨌 **QA Gate** 쨌 UX/UI Consistency Pass 쨌 Finish)瑜?plan??紐낆떆?곸쑝濡???꺼????쒕떎.   33: 
  34: For very small changes this can be a short checklist in the agent response. For larger work, save the plan under `docs/ai-workflow/plans/` and follow that folder's `README.md` for naming and required sections.
  35: 
  36: ## 1b. Light Spec
  37: 
  38: Phase-sized work requires a 1-page light spec at `docs/ai-workflow/light-specs/phase-{n}-{slug}.md` **before any implementation begins**. The light spec compresses the phase's intent and boundaries; the plan is for task-level decomposition.
  39: 
  40: Six required sections:
  41: 
  42: 1. **Core Functionality** ??1?? core values delivered this phase
  43: 2. **Out of Scope** ??what is intentionally not built and why
  44: 3. **Minimum Acceptable Behavior** ??smallest set of conditions for "it works"
  45: 4. **User Flow** ??one-line entry-to-exit path, referencing `docs/flow/user-flow.md`
  46: 5. **Domain Boundary** ??domain handled, its target folder (`docs/domain-glossary.md`), and **`Audience: user 쨌 admin 쨌 both`** (??phase ?붾㈃/沅뚰븳 紐⑤뜽???????UI/沅뚰븳 遺꾧린 ?쒖젙. 鍮꾨??뷀삎 `cron 쨌 system 쨌 external partner`??蹂꾨룄 異뺤쑝濡?異뷀썑 ?꾩엯). `both`?대㈃ user/admin 媛곴컖??遺꾧린 寃쎄퀎? ????대뜑(?? `src/app/admin/...` vs `src/app/library/...`, `src/lib/admin/`, `src/lib/auth/admin-guard.ts`)瑜???以꾩뵫 紐낆떆?쒕떎. Audience ?꾨뱶??[`review-gates.md`](review-gates.md) Architecture Pass??"audience 寃쎄퀎 = 肄붾뱶 boundary ?쇱튂" 寃利앹쓽 ?낅젰???쒕떎. **?쒖? 6?뱀뀡???곕Ⅴ吏 ?딅뒗 湲곗〈 light spec**(?? `phase-6-admin-library-hardening.md`)? 蹂꾨룄 `## Audience` ?뱀뀡?쇰줈 ?숈씪 ?뺣낫瑜??쒓린?대룄 ?덉슜?쒕떎. ?좉퇋 light spec? 蹂?5踰???ぉ ?덉뿉 ??以꾨줈 紐낆떆??寃?
  47: 6. **Success Criteria** ??how the phase will be closed
  48: 
  49: The corresponding ledger must reference it:
  50: 
  51: ```
  52: - Light Spec: docs/ai-workflow/light-specs/phase-4-learning-core.md
  53: ```
  54: 
  55: `scripts/ai-workflow-check.mjs` requires this line whenever the ledger is a phase ledger (filename contains `phase-N` or body has `Phase: ...`). Light specs are not required for tiny docs/config or single-task work.
  56: 
  57: ## Plan Template Contract (machine-checked)
  58: 
  59: Every plan under `docs/ai-workflow/plans/` (other than `README.md` and `*-template.md`) must contain these sections non-empty:
  60: 
  61: - `## Out of Scope ??Intentional Cuts` ??items intentionally excluded with reasons; forces subtraction discipline
  62: - `## Smallest Buildable Unit` ??smallest slice of the plan that is independently buildable
  63: 
  64: If a `## Tasks` section exists, its task table must include a `Subagent-eligible? (Y/N + reason)` column and every data row must give a `Y ??<reason>` or `N ??<reason>` value (em-dash or hyphen accepted). When the phase Audience is `both`, the table must also include an `Audience` column with `user | admin | both | n/a` per row so child agents receive the right boundary in their Task Packet. Phases whose Audience is `user` or `admin` (single-value) may omit the column.
  65: 
  66: ```text
  67: | # | Task | Files | Audience | Subagent-eligible? (Y/N + reason) |
  68: | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  69: | 1 | DB schema | src/db/*.ts | n/a | Y ??independent module |
  70: | 2 | Admin role-change RPC | supabase/migrations/*.sql | admin | Y ??server-only, isolated |
  71: | 3 | Library UI | src/app/library/*.tsx | user | N ??depends on Task 1 result |
  72: ```
  73: 
  74: Phase plans named `*-development-phases-and-bootstrap.md` are additionally checked: every Phase Contract row's `Completion Gate` cell must mention `Architecture Pass`. CI blocks PRs that violate any of the above.
  75: 
  76: ## When Scope Changes (lesson from cleanup PR)
  77: 
  78: If a pre-review surfaces scope reduction (e.g., FAIL because a task is too broad), update **every layer** of the plan in the same revision:
  79: 
  80: - Scope summary at top
  81: - Out of Scope table
  82: - Task table
  83: - Per-task detail sections
  84: - Architecture description
  85: - Risks list
  86: - Verification Strategy
  87: 
  88: A single-layer fix is the most common cause of repeated FAIL/CONCERN rounds. The reviewer will catch the next inconsistent layer if any is left.
  89: 
  90: ## Related
  91: 
  92: - Plan review and re-review gate ??[`review-gates.md`](review-gates.md)
  93: - Ledger that records the plan's decisions and evidence ??[`context-and-packets.md`](context-and-packets.md)
  94: - Failure classes when a planning gate is blocked ??[`fallback-and-recovery.md`](fallback-and-recovery.md)

  25: export const dynamic = "force-dynamic";
  26: 
  27: type VerifyType = "signup" | "recovery" | "email_change" | "email";
  28: const ALLOWED_VERIFY_TYPES = new Set<VerifyType>([
  29:   "signup",
  30:   "recovery",
  31:   "email_change",
  32:   "email",
  33: ]);
  34: 
  35: function isVerifyType(value: string | null): value is VerifyType {
  36:   return value !== null && ALLOWED_VERIFY_TYPES.has(value as VerifyType);
  37: }
  38: 
  39: function buildErrorUrl(
  40:   request: NextRequest,
  41:   reason: string,
  42:   retryAfterSeconds: number | null,
  43: ): URL {
  44:   const url = new URL("/auth/error", request.url);
  45:   url.searchParams.set("reason", reason);
  46:   if (retryAfterSeconds !== null) {
  47:     url.searchParams.set("retry_after_seconds", String(retryAfterSeconds));
  48:   }
  49:   return url;
  50: }
  51: 
  52: export async function GET(request: NextRequest) {
  53:   const { searchParams } = new URL(request.url);
  54:   const next = sanitizeNext(searchParams.get("next"));
  55: 
  56:   // 1. Provider error in query (some OAuth providers).
  57:   const errorCode = searchParams.get("error_code");
  58:   const errorDescription = searchParams.get("error_description");
  59:   if (errorCode) {
  60:     console.error("[auth/callback] provider error in query", {
  61:       errorCode,
  62:       errorDescription,
  63:     });
  64:     return NextResponse.redirect(
  65:       buildErrorUrl(request, mapSupabaseErrorCode(errorCode), null),
  66:     );
  67:   }
  68: 
  69:   const tokenHash = searchParams.get("token_hash");
  70:   const typeParam = searchParams.get("type");
  71:   const code = searchParams.get("code");
  72: 
  73:   // 2a. token_hash ?덉?留?type??invalid ??malformed callback. Codex ?꾩냽:
  74:   // fragment fallback?쇰줈 ?섎━吏 留먭퀬 紐낆떆?곸쑝濡?/auth/error?reason=unknown.
  75:   if (tokenHash && !isVerifyType(typeParam)) {
  76:     console.error("[auth/callback] malformed callback: token_hash present but invalid type", {
  77:       typeParam,
  78:     });
  79:     return NextResponse.redirect(buildErrorUrl(request, "unknown", null));
  80:   }
  81: 
  82:   // 2. Server-side PKCE main flow.
  83:   if (tokenHash && isVerifyType(typeParam)) {
  84:     const supabase = await createSupabaseServerClient();
  85:     const { error } = await supabase.auth.verifyOtp({
  86:       token_hash: tokenHash,
  87:       type: typeParam,
  88:     });
  89:     if (error) {
  90:       console.error("[auth/callback] verifyOtp error", {
  91:         code: error.code,
  92:         message: error.message,
  93:         status: error.status,
  94:       });
  95:       return NextResponse.redirect(
  96:         buildErrorUrl(request, mapSupabaseErrorCode(error.code), null),
  97:       );
  98:     }
  99:     // ?몄뀡 荑좏궎??createSupabaseServerClient??setAll callback??cookies().set?쇰줈
 100:     // ?깅줉 ??Route Handler ?묐떟??Set-Cookie ?ㅻ뜑 emit??
 101:     return NextResponse.redirect(new URL(next, request.url));
 102:   }
 103: 
 104:   // 3. OAuth code flow.
 105:   if (code) {
 106:     const supabase = await createSupabaseServerClient();
 107:     const { error } = await supabase.auth.exchangeCodeForSession(code);
 108:     if (error) {
 109:       console.error("[auth/callback] exchangeCodeForSession error", {
 110:         code: error.code,
 111:         message: error.message,
 112:         status: error.status,
 113:       });
 114:       return NextResponse.redirect(
 115:         buildErrorUrl(request, mapSupabaseErrorCode(error.code), null),
 116:       );
 117:     }
 118:     return NextResponse.redirect(new URL(next, request.url));
 119:   }
 120: 
 121:   // 4. No server-readable token ??implicit flow with fragment.
 122:   // RFC 7231: Location ?ㅻ뜑??fragment ?놁쑝硫?user agent媛 ?먮옒 URL??fragment瑜?  // ??location??retain. ?곕씪???곕━媛 query ?놁씠 redirect?섎㈃ 釉뚮씪?곗?媛 ?먮룞?쇰줈
 123:   // #error_code=... ?먮뒗 #access_token=... ????path??遺숈뿬???곕씪媛꾨떎.
 124:   // /auth/callback-fragment page媛 client component濡?window.location.hash ?뚯떛.
 125:   const fragmentTarget = new URL("/auth/callback-fragment", request.url);
 126:   fragmentTarget.searchParams.set("next", next);
 127:   return NextResponse.redirect(fragmentTarget);
 128: }

   1: // Phase 7-B (Plan rev3 Task 1, R-10 mitigation).
   2: //
   3: // Single builder for all Supabase Auth redirects (signUp emailRedirectTo,
   4: // signInWithOtp emailRedirectTo, resetPasswordForEmail redirectTo).
   5: // Always returns an absolute http(s) URL; never a bare relative path.
   6: 
   7: const DEV_FALLBACK = "http://127.0.0.1:3000";
   8: 
   9: function ensureLeadingSlash(path: string): string {
  10:   return path.startsWith("/") ? path : `/${path}`;
  11: }
  12: 
  13: function stripTrailingSlash(url: string): string {
  14:   return url.endsWith("/") ? url.slice(0, -1) : url;
  15: }
  16: 
  17: function resolveSiteUrl(): string {
  18:   const env = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL?.trim();
  19:   if (env && env.length > 0) {
  20:     // Validate scheme ??reject javascript:, data:, etc.
  21:     if (!/^https?:\/\//i.test(env)) {
  22:       throw new Error(
  23:         `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL must start with http:// or https://, got: ${env}`,
  24:       );
  25:     }
  26:     return stripTrailingSlash(env);
  27:   }
  28: 
  29:   if (process.env.NODE_ENV === "development") {
  30:     return DEV_FALLBACK;
  31:   }
  32: 
  33:   throw new Error(
  34:     "NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL is required in non-development environments",
  35:   );
  36: }
  37: 
  38: export function buildAuthRedirectUrl(path: string): string {
  39:   const site = resolveSiteUrl();
  40:   return `${site}${ensureLeadingSlash(path)}`;
  41: }

   1: # Fallback And Recovery
   2: 
   3: Failure classification and fallback matrix. **Entry point**: [`docs/ai-development-workflow.md`](../ai-development-workflow.md).
   4: 
   5: This sub-doc owns 짠3e (Fallback And Recovery Protocol) of the legacy workflow.
   6: 
   7: ## Principle
   8: 
   9: Fallback is not permission to skip a quality gate. It is a controlled way to either recover equivalent evidence through another path or stop safely with a clear blocker report.
  10: 
  11: ## Failure Classes
  12: 
  13: Classify the failure first:
  14: 
  15: | Failure class | Examples | Required response |
  16: | --- | --- | --- |
  17: | **Fail closed** | Doc conflict, destructive action, secret exposure risk, security uncertainty, missing user approval, **audience-mismatch** (Light Spec `Audience: user`?몃뜲 admin RPC/媛???몄텧???꾩슂?댁쭚, ?먮뒗 `Audience: admin`?몃뜲 ?쇰컲 ?ъ슜??RLS 寃쎈줈瑜?嫄대뱶由? ?먮뒗 child agent??`Audience verified: no` 寃곌낵) | Stop. Report the blocker, exact references, and what decision/input is needed. Do not implement. |
  18: | **Degraded mode** | GStack unavailable, browser automation unavailable, test runner missing, only one AI available | Use the closest equivalent manual or local checklist. Record degraded mode and evidence in the ledger / final report. |
  19: | **Recover** | Context compaction, missing/stale ledger, interrupted session, child result missing context | Rebuild context from `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`, [entry workflow doc](../ai-development-workflow.md), run ledger, consulted docs, and current files before continuing. |
  20: | **Retry once** | Transient network error, temporary CLI failure, flaky command | Retry once after checking the command and environment. If it fails again, stop and report the command, output, and next required action. |
  21: | **Reassign** | Child agent timeout, child agent did not return a result packet, overlapping write scope | Main session reclaims the scope or re-delegates with a tighter task packet. Completion blocked until a result packet or direct verification exists. |
  22: 
  23: ## Fallback Matrix
  24: 
  25: | Normal requirement | Accepted fallback | Not acceptable |
  26: | --- | --- | --- |
  27: | Relevant docs must be read | If a specific doc is missing, state it, read the closest active doc, and use office-hours/brainstorming plus approval gate for net-new scope | Inventing requirements |
  28: | Superpowers skill invocation | Read the project-local skill file directly and follow the closest applicable procedure | Ignoring the skill because the host tool is unavailable |
  29: | GStack review | Run the equivalent review checklist manually and record degraded mode | Skipping review without explanation |
  30: | TDD | If no runnable test surface exists, state the exception and use static inspection, build/typecheck/lint, or manual verification | Writing behavior code with no test or verification note |
  31: | Browser/visual QA | Manual flow checklist, screenshots when available, responsive inspection, or documented blocker | Claiming UI works without any flow evidence |
  32: | Multi-agent reviewer | Same agent performs review gate with explicit self-review checklist, or reassigns to available reviewer | Treating implementation as reviewed |
  33: | Child result packet | Ask for the result packet again, or main session directly inspects files and verification | Trusting hidden child context |
  34: | GitHub/network push | Keep local commit, report remote failure, command output, and retry command | Claiming publication succeeded before remote confirmation |
  35: | Cross-model review | Single model self-reviews with explicit checklist; ledger records `Cross-model review: degraded ??<reason>` | Skipping cross-model review without ledger record |
  36: | Plan-review PASS gate (re-review) | After FAIL revision, the same model re-reviews; if model unavailable, ledger records `Re-review: degraded ??<reason>` and accepts residual risk | Treating revised plan as PASS without re-review |
  37: 
  38: ## Final Report Fallback Section
  39: 
  40: When any fallback path was used, the final report must include:
  41: 
  42: - Normal path that failed
  43: - Failure class (fail-closed / degraded-mode / recover / retry-once / reassign)
  44: - Fallback used
  45: - Evidence collected
  46: - Remaining risk
  47: - Whether completion is allowed or blocked
  48: 
  49: If the fallback is `fail-closed`, the report must state what input or decision is needed from the user before work can resume.
  50: 
  51: ## Fail-Closed Triggers
  52: 
  53: Stop and request user input ??do not implement ??when any of these is true:
  54: 
  55: - Active docs conflict with the user request
  56: - A destructive action is implied (force push, schema drop, secret rotation, mass file delete)
  57: - A secret exposure risk is present
  58: - Security-relevant behavior is uncertain
  59: - A required user approval is missing
  60: - The plan-review PASS gate has not closed and the implementer is the same model that wrote the plan
  61: - **Audience-mismatch**: Light Spec `Audience` 媛믨낵 ?ㅼ젣 ?묒뾽???꾩슂濡??섎뒗 沅뚰븳 紐⑤뜽???닿툔??(?? `user` phase?몃뜲 admin guard ?몄텧???꾩슂, `admin` phase?몃뜲 ?쇰컲 ?ъ슜??RLS 寃쎈줈瑜?留뚯쭚). ?ъ슜?먭? audience 媛깆떊???뱀씤?섍굅???묒뾽???ㅼ쓬 phase濡?誘몃（湲??꾧퉴吏 肄붾뱶 蹂寃?湲덉?
  62: 
  63: ## Related
  64: 
  65: - Ledger fields that record degraded mode and fallback evidence ??[`context-and-packets.md`](context-and-packets.md)
  66: - Review gates that may invoke degraded mode ??[`review-gates.md`](review-gates.md)
  67: - Planning contracts that govern when fallback is acceptable for plan/light-spec work ??[`planning-contracts.md`](planning-contracts.md)

   1: # AI Development Workflow
   2: 
   3: Entry point for every AI agent working in this repository. **Read this file first, then follow the links to the sub-doc that governs your current task.** Detailed rules live in the four sub-docs listed at the bottom ??they are not duplicated here.
   4: 
   5: ## Mandatory Startup
   6: 
   7: 1. Invoke `using-superpowers` (Claude Code) or read its canonical SKILL file (other hosts). If host mirrors are stale, run `node scripts/sync-agent-skills.mjs` and retry.
   8: 2. Read [`docs/agent-index.md`](agent-index.md), classify your goal, and select the smallest matching docs.
   9: 3. Record `Docs consulted`, `Extracted requirements`, `Doc conflicts`, `Untouched relevant docs`, and `Context ledger` in your plan or ledger.
  10: 4. Run `node scripts/ai-workflow-check.mjs --repo .` before final reporting when Node is available. PRs run the same checker via `.github/workflows/ai-workflow-check.yml`.
  11: 
  12: ## Workflow Diagram
  13: 
  14: ```mermaid
  15: flowchart TD
  16:   A["Task starts"] --> B["Use Superpowers"]
  17:   B --> C["Read agent-index"]
  18:   C --> D["Select docs"]
  19:   D --> E["Record evidence"]
  20:   E --> F{"Covered by docs?"}
  21:   F -->|"No"| X["Proposal or approved brief"]
  22:   F -->|"Conflict"| Y["Stop with references"]
  23:   F -->|"Yes"| G{"Lane"}
  24:   G -->|"Tiny docs/config"| L["Lightweight path"]
  25:   G -->|"Implementation"| P["Plan + TDD"]
  26:   G -->|"UI/flow"| U["Plan + design QA"]
  27:   P --> R["Review gates"]
  28:   U --> R
  29:   L --> V["Narrow verification"]
  30:   R --> V
  31:   V --> W["Workflow checker"]
  32:   W --> Z["Final report"]
  33: ```
  34: 
  35: ## Lane Selection
  36: 
  37: | Work type | First action | Rules live in |
  38: | --- | --- | --- |
  39: | Tiny docs/config, no behavior change | Lightweight Path below | this file 짠Lightweight |
  40: | Bug fix | `systematic-debugging` ??TDD | [`review-gates.md`](ai-workflow/review-gates.md) |
  41: | Feature / behavior change | `writing-plans` ??TDD ??review gates | [`planning-contracts.md`](ai-workflow/planning-contracts.md), [`review-gates.md`](ai-workflow/review-gates.md) |
  42: | UI / user flow | `writing-plans` + design review ??TDD ??UX/UI Consistency Pass ??audience蹂?QA | same as above + design review; Audience rules ?꾨옒 李몄“; [`review-gates.md#uxui-consistency-pass`](ai-workflow/review-gates.md#uxui-consistency-pass) |
  43: | Net-new scope or doc pivot | `office-hours` + `brainstorming` ??docs update proposal OR approved brief | [`planning-contracts.md`](ai-workflow/planning-contracts.md) |
  44: | Conflict with active docs | Stop. Report conflict with exact references. Do not implement. | this file 짠Mandatory Startup |
  45: | Multi-agent / phase work | Plan + Light Spec + Ledger + cross-model review | [`planning-contracts.md`](ai-workflow/planning-contracts.md), [`context-and-packets.md`](ai-workflow/context-and-packets.md) |
  46:

  56: 
  57: ## Core Invariants
  58: 
  59: These are mandatory for any non-lightweight change. **The linked sub-doc is the authoritative source** ??this list exists so an agent reading only the entry file does not miss them.
  60: 
  61: - **TDD** (RED ??confirm fail ??GREEN ??confirm pass ??refactor while green). Allowed exceptions are docs-only, config-only, generated artifacts, or no runnable test surface. Full loop and exceptions: [`review-gates.md#tdd`](ai-workflow/review-gates.md).
  62: - **Cross-model review is mandatory** for every code change and every non-trivial plan or doc change. When only one model is available, record `Cross-model review: degraded ??<reason>` in the ledger. [`review-gates.md#cross-model-review`](ai-workflow/review-gates.md#cross-model-review).
  63: - **Plan-Review PASS Gate** ??if a pre-implementation review (`plan-eng-review`, `codex consult`, etc) returns FAIL, revise the plan AND re-run the same review until PASS or until each remaining concern is recorded as "accepted with reason" in the ledger. [`review-gates.md#plan-review-pass-gate`](ai-workflow/review-gates.md).
  64: - **Architecture Pass** at phase completion: route handlers have no leaked business logic, folder/name boundaries match `docs/domain-glossary.md`, no single concept is implemented in two places. [`review-gates.md#architecture-pass`](ai-workflow/review-gates.md).
  65: - **UX/UI Consistency Pass** when changed files match UI patterns (`src/app/**`, `src/components/**`, `src/features/**`, `src/lib/ui/**`, `src/styles/**`, `*.css`, `theme*`, etc.). 4-line evidence(Tokens 쨌 Components 쨌 A11y 쨌 Responsive) in ledger, machine-checked. Test-only changes auto-exempt. [`review-gates.md#uxui-consistency-pass`](ai-workflow/review-gates.md).
  66: - **CSS Variable Scoping Gate** when changed files include `src/theme/**`, `src/styles/**`, `app/layout.tsx`, or any file containing `--app-*` declarations: verify the five constraints in [`docs/ant-design/06-ai-development-workflow.md#css-variable-scoping`](ant-design/06-ai-development-workflow.md) and run `scripts/ai-workflow-check.mjs`. The gate is machine-enforced for the following patterns: `--app-*: var(--ant-*)` chains, bare `@theme {` without `inline`, and `getAppTheme` at module scope. [`docs/ant-design/08-theme-architecture.md#css-variable-architecture-contract`](ant-design/08-theme-architecture.md).
  67: - **Light Spec + Out of Scope + Smallest Buildable Unit + Subagent-eligible column** are mandatory plan/light-spec sections, machine-checked by `scripts/ai-workflow-check.mjs`. [`planning-contracts.md`](ai-workflow/planning-contracts.md).
  68: - **Context ledger** is required for any non-trivial work (multi-file, implementation, UI/route/auth/database/API/dependency/test-strategy/AI-boundary change, multi-agent work, work likely to resume across sessions, **or any change to workflow-governing files ??`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/agent-index.md`, `docs/ai-development-workflow.md`, files under `docs/ai-workflow/`, `scripts/`, `.github/`**). [`context-and-packets.md`](ai-workflow/context-and-packets.md).
  69: - **Fallback Protocol** ??fallback never weakens a quality gate. Classify failures (fail-closed, degraded-mode, recover, retry-once, reassign) and record evidence in the ledger. [`fallback-and-recovery.md`](ai-workflow/fallback-and-recovery.md).
  70: 
  71: ## Required Evidence (Before Final Report)
  72: 
  73: - `Docs consulted` ??exact files read

  82: 
  83: ## Lightweight Path
  84: 
  85: For a tiny docs/config/non-behavioral change with no multi-agent work, no UI/flow change, no doc conflict, and no resume risk:
  86: 
  87: 1. `using-superpowers`
  88: 2. Skill applicability check
  89: 3. Edit
  90: 4. Narrowest relevant verification (lint/typecheck/inspection)
  91: 5. Report checks and risks
  92: 
  93: A context ledger may be skipped only if every condition above is satisfied. State the exception in the final report. This path is **not allowed for production behavior changes**.

docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:7:## TDD
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:20:### Allowed TDD exceptions
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:31:**Cross-model review is mandatory** for every code change and every non-trivial plan or doc change. A different model than the implementer must read the diff and either record findings or sign off. The intent is to cover one model's blind spots with another model's reading.
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:38:When only one model is available, the implementer must record `Cross-model review: degraded — <reason>` in the ledger's `## Verification State`. The checker (`scripts/ai-workflow-check.mjs`) treats `Cross-model review:` as required; missing/empty values block CI. Degraded completion is allowed only when the reason is recorded.
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:42:## Plan-Review PASS Gate
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:48:3. Only proceed to implementation after the re-review returns **PASS** (or **CONCERN** with each remaining concern explicitly documented in the ledger as "accepted with reason")
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:50:Going straight from "FAIL → revise → implement" without a re-review is a workflow violation: the implementer is signing off on their own revision and the originally-flagged risks have not been re-validated by an independent reader. This is true even when post-implementation cross-model review is planned — that catches code-level issues, not plan-level ones.
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:52:Record the pre-implementation review chain in the ledger Decisions table with timestamps for the original review, the revision, and the re-review. If only one model is available for re-review, record `Re-review: degraded — <reason>` and accept the residual risk explicitly.
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:58:- **CONCERN verdict** on the first review needs the concerns documented and accepted, but no full re-review unless the implementer changes the plan in response
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:60:### Round-cap rule (lesson from cleanup PR)
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:94:When an independent reviewer or GStack review is unavailable, record degraded mode and run an explicit self-review checklist:
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:98:- Tests: changed behavior covered, or TDD exception + substitute verification documented
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:207:- QA Gate: passed | failed | degraded — <blocker + alternative verification + residual risk> | skipped — <reason>
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:213:- **degraded**: 정상 실행 불가(예: 브라우저 자동화 없음, env dependency 결손, 원격 dependency 다운). `degraded` 단독은 불충분 — **반드시 (a) blocker (b) 대체 검증 수단 (c) 잔여 위험** 셋을 한 줄에 명시. release/phase 완료는 degraded면 원칙적으로 막힘(fail-closed). 사용자/owner가 명시 승인한 경우에만 진행 가능.
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:221:- `degraded` 단독(파이프 분리된 blocker/alternative/residual risk 명시 없음)은 FAIL.
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:226:phase ledger의 `Status: complete` 또는 phase plan의 final report 시점에 `QA Gate: degraded`가 있으면 자동 점검은 PASS 안 줌. 사용자/owner 승인 후 ledger에 "QA Gate degraded accepted by <owner> — <date>" 한 줄을 별도로 박아야 통과.
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:228:### degraded 처리 정신
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:230:`degraded`는 **통과권이 아니라 위험 라벨**이다. "기록하면 통과"가 아니라 "기록해서 위험을 숨길 수 없게 함" — 본 사고의 직접 교훈.
docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:255:- Ledger that records review evidence and degraded status → [`context-and-packets.md`](context-and-packets.md)

### 1. TDD exception justification
Verdict: CONCERN  
Finding: 계획은 `Documentation-only changes` 예외라고 쓰지만, 실제 변경 범위에는 `.env.example`도 있습니다(`docs/ai-workflow/plans/20260527-1600-auth-overview-codex-fix.md:3`, `:228-238`). 다만 규칙상 문서-only뿐 아니라 config-only도 TDD 예외입니다(`docs/ai-workflow/review-gates.md:20-27`). `.env.example` 현재 1-20행은 예시 설정 파일이고, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`이 없으며(`.env.example:8-20`), 실제 런타임 throw는 `redirect-url.ts:33-35`에 있습니다.  
Suggested fix: `Documentation-only changes`를 `Documentation/config-only changes`로 바꾸고, substitute verification에 “config example static inspection”을 명시하세요.

### 2. Smallest Buildable Unit choice
Verdict: PASS  
Finding: P0 묶음은 `auth-overview.md`의 거짓 참조와 `.env.example` 누락을 한 번에 닫는 단위입니다(`plan:51-60`). `.env.example` 누락은 `redirect-url.ts`의 non-development throw와 직접 연결됩니다(`src/lib/auth/redirect-url.ts:29-35`), 현재 `.env.example`에는 값이 없습니다(`.env.example:8-20`).  
Suggested fix: 없음.

### 3. Out of Scope completeness
Verdict: FAIL  
Finding: PW max 64 구현/IA 수정은 scope 밖으로 자르는 게 가능하지만(`plan:44-45`), Round 1 FAIL #10c 자체는 “충돌을 문서에서 어떻게 닫을지”가 필요합니다(`tasks/codex-output-auth-overview-review-round1.md:82-85`). 실제 IA는 PW 8-64를 요구하고(`docs/IA/01-A-01-sign-up/description.md:58-60`, `docs/IA/28-X-06-password-reset/description.md:52-54`), 구현은 min 8만 검사합니다(`src/components/auth/SignUpForm.tsx:71-77`, `src/components/auth/PasswordResetConfirmForm.tsx:43-49`). 그런데 Edit-by-Edit Table에는 이 drift를 `auth-overview.md`에 반영하는 edit가 없습니다(`plan:188-202`).  
Suggested fix: E11을 추가하세요. 예: `§10 SSoT matrix 또는 별도 note에 "PW 8-64는 IA 요구와 현재 구현(min 8 only)이 drift 상태다. 이번 작업은 구현/IA 변경 없이 drift 기록만 한다." 추가`.

### 4. Task table Subagent-eligible classification
Verdict: CONCERN  
Finding: T4는 두 파일 변경입니다(`plan:85`, `:156-185`). 그런데 eligible 사유가 “단일 파일 다중 라인 패치” 취지로 읽혀 실제 scope와 어긋납니다. ground truth도 두 파일 상태 확인이 필요합니다: `auth-overview.md`의 callback/reset/env 문구(`docs/development/auth-overview.md:63`, `:103`, `:203`)와 `.env.example` 누락(`.env.example:8-20`).  
Suggested fix: T4 사유를 `Y — two-file bounded docs/config patch with exact read/write scope`로 바꾸거나, 더 보수적으로 `T4a auth-overview.md`, `T4b .env.example`로 쪼개세요.

### 5. Edit-by-Edit Table accuracy
Verdict: CONCERN  
Finding: E2, E4, E10의 핵심 방향은 맞습니다. 현재 문서는 없는 `callback/page.tsx`를 가리키고(`docs/development/auth-overview.md:63`, `:108`), 실제 파일은 `route.ts`와 `callback-fragment/page.tsx`입니다(`src/app/auth/callback/route.ts:52-127`, `src/app/auth/callback-fragment/page.tsx:23-35`). reset도 현재 문서는 `/auth/callback` 경유라고 쓰지만(`auth-overview.md:102-104`), 실제 코드는 confirm으로 직접 보냅니다(`PasswordResetRequestForm.tsx:21-23`). 다만 E7은 “`.env.example:15-16에도 탑재`”라고 쓰는데, 현재 15행은 `ACCESS_TOKEN`이고 `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`은 없습니다(`.env.example:15-20`).  
Suggested fix: E7 정정 문구를 “E10 적용 후 `.env.example`에 추가된다”로 바꾸세요. 현재 존재하는 것처럼 쓰면 안 됩니다.

### 6. 3-Layer Gate design
Verdict: PASS  
Finding: T0/T2는 plan re-review, T3는 fresh ratify, T6는 post-implementation review로 분리되어 있습니다(`plan:129-152`, `:211-219`, `:279-289`). 이는 PASS gate가 post-implementation review로 대체될 수 없다는 규칙과도 맞습니다(`review-gates.md:42-52`).  
Suggested fix: 없음. T3는 T0와 일부 중복되지만, 사용자 명시 요구라 유지가 맞습니다.

### 7. Task Packet (T4) completeness
Verdict: PASS  
Finding: Task Packet 필수 필드는 모두 있습니다(`plan:162-185`), 템플릿 요구와 대응됩니다(`agent-packets.md:5-24`). `Audience: n/a`도 non-UI/non-permission 작업에는 맞습니다(`agent-packets.md:13`, `:27`). Node 검증은 같은 repo sandbox에서 실행 가능한 검증으로 보는 게 타당합니다(`review-gates.md:236-238`).  
Suggested fix: 선택 사항으로, Node가 없을 때 fallback을 `degraded — checker unavailable, grep/manual checklist used, residual risk ...` 형식으로 적어두면 더 단단합니다.

### 8. Round-cap & escalation rules
Verdict: CONCERN  
Finding: 계획은 Round-cap 3을 여러 곳에 언급합니다(`plan:134-135`, `:151-152`, `:216-219`). 하지만 T2 plan-review round, T3 ratify round, T6 post-implementation review round가 각각 독립 카운터인지 불명확합니다. 기본 cap 3과 5 초과 사용자 escalation 규칙은 문서에 있습니다(`review-gates.md:60-65`).  
Suggested fix: Verification Strategy에 “Plan-review, T3 ratify, T6 post-impl은 별도 round counter. 각 counter 3회 초과 시 사용자 escalation”을 한 줄 추가하세요.

### 9. Risks completeness
Verdict: CONCERN  
Finding: R1-R6은 유효하지만(`plan:240-249`), fallback 문서가 요구하는 degraded/reassign 경로가 빠져 있습니다(`fallback-and-recovery.md:18-21`, `:35-36`). 특히 Codex CLI unavailable, T3 HOLD가 non-actionable일 때, `.env.example` 추가가 CI/env 정책과 충돌할 때가 빠져 있습니다. E10 자체는 실제 throw와 연결되지만(`redirect-url.ts:33-35`), `.env.example` 추가가 배포 환경에서 “required var”로 해석될 위험도 별도 리스크입니다.  
Suggested fix: R7 Codex CLI unavailable, R8 non-actionable HOLD, R9 `.env.example`/CI env policy collision을 추가하세요.

### 10. Acceptance Criteria measurability
Verdict: CONCERN  
Finding: “9개 edit 모두 grep verifiable”은 과장입니다(`plan:253`). E1, E3, E5, E6, E8/E8b, E9는 각각 고유 grep target이 없습니다. “Codex Round 2 PASS or CONCERN(accepted with reason)”도 ledger에 각 concern을 명시해야 한다는 규칙을 직접 연결해야 합니다(`review-gates.md:48`, `:58`). T3는 계획상 GO만 구현 진입 조건이므로 criterion 자체는 맞습니다(`plan:150-152`, `:260`).  
Suggested fix: 각 edit별 grep/check target 표를 추가하고, “accepted with reason = ledger Decisions 또는 Verification State에 concern별 reason 기록”으로 정의하세요.

- Supabase 공식 문서 확인 결과, OTP 기본값 360/hour와 60초 window는 현재 문서와 맞습니다【turn1view1†L142-L150】. refresh token 설명도 “기본 세션 무기한, refresh token은 만료 없이 1회 사용”이 맞습니다【turn1view0†L127-L130】.
- Docs consulted: `using-superpowers`, `docs/agent-index.md`, `docs/ai-development-workflow.md`, `planning-contracts.md`, `review-gates.md`, `agent-packets.md`, `fallback-and-recovery.md`, plan file, Round 1 output/report, `auth-overview.md`, listed source/config/test files, Supabase official docs.
- Context ledger: 이번 응답은 review-only 산출물이라 새 파일은 만들지 않았습니다.
